<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Let <i>h</i> be a fixed integer polynomial of degree at least two with positive leading coefficient, having a unit root modulo every positive integer. We prove that any set $`A\subseteq\{1,\ldots,N\}`$ whose differences avoid all nonzero values $`h(p)`$ at primes satisfies $`|A|\le C_hN^{1-c_h}`$, where $`c_h\gt 0`$ and $`C_h\ge1`$ depend only on <i>h</i>. Thus the local unit-root condition gives a fixed power saving even when polynomial arguments are restricted to primes. The proof uses the companion zero-free half-plane theorem for Dirichlet <i>L</i>-functions to obtain the required prime-distribution estimates.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $h\in\mathbb Z[x]$ be prime-intersective, of degree at least two and with positive leading coefficient. There exist constants $c_h>0$ and $C_h\ge1$ such that, for every positive integer $N$ and every $A\subseteq[N]$, $$(A-A)\cap\{h(p):p\in\mathbb P\}\subseteq\{0\} \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \|A\|\le C_h N^{1-c_h}.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the size of sets avoiding nonzero polynomial values at prime arguments.
- **C2** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-auxiliary)): For every $l\ge1$, the polynomial $h_l$ belongs to $\mathbb Z[x]$, has degree $k$ and positive leading coefficient $a_l$, and all its coefficients are $O_h(a_l)$. We have $h_1=h$ and $T_1(x)=x$. For every $D\ge1$, there is an integer $i\in\{0,\ldots,D-1\}$ such that $$\label{eq:auxiliary-change} \begin{split} T_l(Dx-i)&=T_{lD}(x),\\ h_l(Dx-i)&=\lambda(D)h_{lD}(x),\qquad D\mid\lambda(D),\quad \lambda(D)\le D^k. \end{split}$$ Moreover, $b_{m_p,p}$ is a $p$-adic unit for all sufficiently large $p$, with a threshold depending only on $h$.
  - Explanation: Builds integral auxiliary polynomials compatible with progression restriction.
- **C3** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-sums)): There is an integer $P_1>\max(k,2)$, depending only on $h$, such that for every $l\ge1$ and every prime $p\ge P_1$, at least $p/2$ arguments in $\mathbb F_p$ are allowed regular. For every integer $v$ with $p\nmid v$, $$\label{eq:local-sums} \begin{split} \left\|\mathbb E_{\substack{x\bmod p^j\\p\nmid T_l(x)}} e\left(\frac{vF(x)}{p^j}\right)\right\| &\le p^{-8\delta j} \qquad(j\ge1),\\ \left\|\mathbb E_{\substack{x\bmod p\\x\text{ allowed regular}}} e\left(\frac{vF(x)}p\right)\right\| &\le p^{-4\delta}. \end{split}$$ Each expectation is normalized by the number of arguments in its stated averaging set.
  - Explanation: Controls local exponential sums uniformly in the auxiliary parameter.
- **C4** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-local-lifting)): For every prime $p<P_1$, there is an integer $S_p\ge0$, depending only on $h$ and $p$, such that, for every $l\ge1$, some integer $x$ satisfies $$\label{eq:small-prime-argument} p\nmid T_l(x),\qquad v_p(h_l'(x))\le S_p.$$ Set $$\label{eq:local-modulus-exponents} E_p=2S_p+1\quad(p<P_1),\qquad E_p=1\quad(p\ge P_1).$$ For any prime $p$, let $x$ be an integer satisfying <a href="#eq:small-prime-argument" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:small-prime-argument">[eq:small-prime-argument]</a> when $p<P_1$, or representing an allowed regular argument when $p\ge P_1$. Write $s=v_p(h_l'(x))$. Every integer in the class $x\pmod{p^{E_p-s}}$ satisfies the unit condition, and its $h_l$-value is congruent to $h_l(x)$ modulo $p^{E_p}$. For each $j>E_p$, the uniform distribution on this argument class modulo $p^j$ pushes forward under $h_l$ to the uniform distribution on $$\{y\bmod p^j:y\equiv h_l(x)\pmod{p^{E_p}}\}.$$
  - Explanation: Provides unit argument classes whose polynomial values lift uniformly.
- **C5** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-admissible-cycle)): Fix an integer $L>2$ such that $$\label{eq:cycle-length} 4\delta L>2,\qquad p^{E_p}\mid L\quad(p<P_1).$$ For every $l\ge1$ and every prime $p$, there are $L$ admissible residues $y_0,\ldots,y_{L-1}$ modulo $p^{E_p}$, with repetitions allowed, such that $$y_0+\cdots+y_{L-1}=0\pmod{p^{E_p}}.$$
  - Explanation: Produces admissible local increments forming a closed cycle.
- **C6** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-tuple-laws)): There is a threshold $P>P_1$, depending only on $h$, with the following property. For every $l\ge1$ and prime $p\ge P$, there are a probability measure $\mu_{l,p}$ on $\mathbb F_p^V$ and a submeasure $0\le\varepsilon_{l,p}\le\mu_{l,p}$ such that:* 1. *$\mu_{l,p}$ is reflection positive. Both $\mu_{l,p}$ and $\varepsilon_{l,p}$ are invariant under simultaneous translation of all labels. In particular, any nonzero one of these measures, or their difference, has uniform single-slot marginals after normalization.* 2. *Writing $$\eta_{l,p}=\varepsilon_{l,p}(1),\qquad \mu_{l,p}^{\circ}=\frac{\mu_{l,p}-\varepsilon_{l,p}}{1-\eta_{l,p}},$$ we have $\eta_{l,p}\le p^{-4}$. Under $\mu_{l,p}^{\circ}$ every increment $z_{v_{j+1}}-z_{v_j}$, $0\le j<L$, is admissible at $l$.* 3. *For any integer $D\ge1$ with $p\nmid D$ and any $a'\in\mathbb F_p$, let $A_{D,a'}$ act on all labels by $z_v\mapsto\lambda(D)^{-1}(z_v-a')$. Then $$\label{eq:tuple-covariance} (A_{D,a'})_*\mu_{l,p}=\mu_{lD,p},\qquad (A_{D,a'})_*\varepsilon_{l,p}=\varepsilon_{lD,p}.$$* 4. *For $\rho_p=\mu_{l,p}$ or $\mu_{l,p}^{\circ}$, every $v\in V$, and every $f:\mathbb F_p\to\mathbb C$ of uniform mean zero, $$\label{eq:tuple-mixing} \left\\|\mathbb E_{\rho_p} \bigl[f(z_v)\mid(z_u)_{u\ne v}\bigr]\right\\|_{L^2(\rho_p)} \le p^{-\gamma}\\|f\\|_{L^2(\mathbb F_p)}.$$* *All thresholds and constants are independent of $l$.
  - Explanation: Constructs reflection-positive tuple laws with admissible cycle increments outside an exceptional submeasure.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-graph-estimates)): Let $G$ be a fixed finite directed graph with no loops and with at most one edge on each unordered pair of vertices. Give its vertices independent uniform labels $Y_x\in\mathbb F_p$, and put $$I_G(Y)=\prod_{x\to y\in E(G)}W(Y_y-Y_x),\qquad e_G=\|E(G)\|.$$ Then $$\label{eq:tuple-graph-mean} \mathbb E I_G=c_p^{e_G}+O_G(p^{-4\delta}).$$ If the vertices are partitioned into two classes and every edge joins different classes, regard $I_G$ as a kernel between their uniform label spaces. Its associated operator satisfies $$\label{eq:tuple-graph-operator} \\|I_G-c_p^{e_G}\\|_{L^2\to L^2}=O_G(p^{-\delta}).$$ Both bounds are uniform in $l$.
  - Explanation: Bounds weighted graph averages and the associated bipartite kernel operators.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-signed-holder)): For every positive integer $l$, finite set $\mathcal P$ of primes at least $P$, and real functions $g,g_v:X_{\mathcal P}\to\mathbb R$, $$Z_{l,\mathcal P}(g)\ge0, \qquad \left\|\mathcal Z_{l,\mathcal P}((g_v)_{v\in V})\right\| \le\prod_{v\in V} Z_{l,\mathcal P}(g_v)^{1/d}. \label{eq:signed-holder}$$
  - Explanation: Deduces nonnegative diagonal integrals and a signed multilinear Hölder inequality.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-seminorm)): The map $g\mapsto\\|g\\|_{l,\mathcal P}$ is a seminorm on the real functions on $X_{\mathcal P}$. It satisfies $$\|\mathbb E g\|\le\\|g\\|_{l,\mathcal P}, \qquad \\|\mathbb E_Bg\\|_{l,\mathcal P\setminus B} =\\|\mathbb E_Bg\\|_{l,\mathcal P} \le\\|g\\|_{l,\mathcal P}.$$ Translations of the input are isometries. Moreover, if $D$ is a positive integer coprime to every prime of $\mathcal P$, then for any $a'\in X_{\mathcal P}$, $$\left\\|g\bigl(\lambda(D)^{-1}(\,\cdot-a')\bigr) \right\\|_{l,\mathcal P} =\\|g\\|_{lD,\mathcal P}. \label{eq:lift-transport}$$ Here the affine change is interpreted separately at each prime.
  - Explanation: Establishes a seminorm with averaging contraction and affine transport.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-large-denominator)): Fix $l\ge1$ and a finite prime set $\mathcal P$ as above. At each prime choose either $\rho_p=\mu_{l,p}$ or $\rho_p=\mu_{l,p}^{\circ}$. Let $g_v:X_{\mathcal P}\to\mathbb C$ be arbitrary functions. If, in one distinguished slot $v$, every Fourier coefficient at a denominator at most $R$ vanishes, where $R\ge1$, then $$\left\|\int\prod_{u\in V}g_u(z_u)\, d\bigotimes_{p\in\mathcal P}\rho_p\right\| \le R^{-\gamma}\\|g_v\\|_2 \prod_{u\ne v}\\|g_u\\|_{2(d-1)}. \label{eq:large-denominator}$$
  - Explanation: Uses conditional mixing and support orthogonality to suppress large Fourier denominators.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-lift-moments)): Let $\mathcal P$ be any finite set of primes, $Q\ge1$, and let $I$ be a consecutive integer interval with $\|I\|\ge10Q^6$. For every integer $r\ge1$, every $\epsilon>0$, every function $f:I\to\mathbb C$, and every subfamily $\mathcal F\subseteq\{B\subseteq\mathcal P:q_B\le Q\}$, $$\left\\|\sum_{B\in\mathcal F}f_B\right\\|_{2r} \le C_{r,\epsilon}\\|f\\|_\infty Q^\epsilon. \label{eq:lift-moments}$$ The constant is independent of the prime set, the interval and its location, the function, and the subfamily.
  - Explanation: Bounds moments of Fourier lifts uniformly over complete support subfamilies.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-lift-specialization)): Let $B\subseteq\mathcal P$, fix $z_B\in X_B$, and let $G:X_{\mathcal P}\to\mathbb C$. For $1\le s<\infty$, $$\\|G(z_B,\cdot)\\|_{L^s(X_{\mathcal P\setminus B})} \le q_B^{1/s}\\|G\\|_{L^s(X_{\mathcal P})}.$$ In particular, a Fourier lift or a subfamily of its complete supports satisfies <a href="#eq:lift-moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lift-moments">[eq:lift-moments]</a> after specialization with the additional factor $q_B^{1/(2r)}$. The same bound holds after retaining either the remaining denominators at most a given $H\ge1$, or those greater than $H$.
  - Explanation: Controls the norm loss from fixing residue coordinates.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-zero-free-input)): For every integer $b\ge1$ and every Dirichlet character $\chi$ modulo $b$, the Dirichlet $L$-function $L(s,\chi)$ has no zero in $\Re s>7/8$. This includes imprimitive characters and the trivial character of modulus $1$, whose $L$-function is $\zeta(s)$. A pole at $s=1$ for a principal character is allowed.
  - Explanation: States the companion zero-free half-plane input, allowing the principal pole.
- **C14** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-prime-progressions)): Uniformly for $Y\ge2$, $0\le x\le Y$, integers $b\ge1$, and reduced residue classes $c$ modulo $b$, $$\label{eq:prime-progression} \sum_{\substack{n\le x\\n\equiv c\pmod b}}\Lambda'(n) =\frac{x}{\varphi(b)} +O\bigl(Y^{31/32}\log^2(2bY)\bigr).$$ For a nonreduced class modulo $b>1$, the sum is $O(\log b)$ and has zero main term.
  - Explanation: Derives uniform prime distribution in reduced residue classes from the zero-free input.
- **C15** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-prime-minor-arcs)): For fixed $k\ge2$ and $0<\rho<1$, there is a constant $c_m=c_m(k,\rho)\in(0,1/4)$ with the following property. If $Y$ is sufficiently large, $I\subseteq[1,Y]$ is an interval, and $f\in\mathbb R[x]$ has degree at most $k$ with coefficient $b_k$ of $x^k$, then $$\label{eq:prime-minor-arc} \left\|\sum_{n\in I}\Lambda'(n)e(f(n))\right\| \ll_{k,\rho}Y^{1-c_m},$$ provided $\theta=(k!)^2b_k$ has an approximation $a/q$ satisfying $$\label{eq:minor-rational-range} (a,q)=1,\qquad \|\theta-a/q\|\le q^{-2},\qquad Y^\rho\le q\le Y^{k-\rho}.$$
  - Explanation: Establishes cancellation for prime polynomial sums under rational approximation hypotheses.
- **C16** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rational-spacing)): Suppose $(a,q)=1$, $q\ge2$, and $\|\theta-a/q\|\le q^{-2}$. For $R\ge1$ and $M\ge1$, $$\label{eq:rational-spacing} \sum_{1\le r\le R}\min(M,\\|\theta r\\|^{-1}) \ll (R/q+1)\bigl(M+q\log(2q)\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Bounds reciprocal circle-distance sums using rational spacing.
- **C17** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-prime-differencing)): Let $k\ge2$, and let $f\in\mathbb R[x]$ have degree at most $k$, with coefficient $b_k$ of $x^k$.* *If $J$ is an interval of integers of length at most an integer $M\ge1$, and $m$ is a positive integer, then $$\label{eq:type-one-differencing} \left\|\frac1M\sum_{n\in J}e(f(mn))\right\|^{2^{k-1}} \ll_k\frac1{M^k} \sum_{\|u_1\|,\ldots,\|u_{k-1}\|<M} \min\bigl(M,\\|k!b_km^ku_1\cdots u_{k-1}\\|^{-1}\bigr).$$* *Next let $A,B$ be positive integers, let $I\subseteq[1,Y]$ be an interval, and let $\|a_m\|,\|d_n\|\le1$. Set $$S=\sum_{\substack{A\le m<2A\\B\le n<2B}} a_md_n\mathbf 1_{\{mn\in I\}}e(f(mn)),\qquad \theta=(k!)^2b_k.$$ Then $$\label{eq:type-two-differencing} \left\|\frac{S}{AB}\right\|^{2^{2k-1}} \ll_k\frac1{A^kB^k} \sum_{\substack{\|u_1\|,\ldots,\|u_k\|<B\\ \|v_1\|,\ldots,\|v_{k-1}\|<A}} \min\bigl(A,\\|\theta u_1\cdots u_kv_1\cdots v_{k-1}\\|^{-1}\bigr).$$ Zero shifts are included in both sums.
  - Explanation: Reduces polynomial exponential sums to linear phases by one-variable and mixed differencing.
- **C18** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-pair-cancellation)): Suppose $N$ is sufficiently large, $l\le N^\zeta$, and $D=M_0\prod_{p\in B}p\le N^\beta$, where $B$ is a finite set of primes at least $P$. Let $J_1,J_2:[N]\to\mathbb C$ have modulus at most one and be supported on residue classes $a'_1,a'_2\pmod D$. Assume that $r=a'_2-a'_1\pmod D$ is admissible at $l$ and that $$J_1(x)J_2(y)\ne0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad x<y\quad\text{and}\quad y-x\notin\mathcal D_l.$$ Set $$\Xi=\{\xi\in\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z:q(\xi)\le H, \ q(\xi)\text{ squarefree},\ (q(\xi),D)=1\}.$$ Then, for the multiplier of any directed cycle edge, $$\label{eq:pair-cancellation} D\left\|\sum_{\xi\in\Xi}\Gamma(\xi) \widehat{J_1}_{[N]}(-\xi)\widehat{J_2}_{[N]}(\xi)\right\| \ll H^{-\gamma/2}.$$
  - Explanation: Converts avoidance on ordered pairs into Fourier cancellation.
- **C19** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-kernel-comparison)): For the data and ranges of Proposition <a href="#prop:pair-cancellation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:pair-cancellation">18</a>, $$\label{eq:kernel-comparison} \sup_{\alpha\in\mathbb R/\mathbb Z}\|S_0(\alpha)-S_1(\alpha)\| \ll H^{-\gamma/2}.$$
  - Explanation: Compares the model and prime-supported kernels uniformly in frequency.
- **C20** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-residue-specialization)): Let $N\ge1$, $A\subseteq[N]$, $s\bmod M_0$, and let $I\subseteq[N]$ be a nonempty consecutive interval. Let $B\subseteq\mathcal P_N$, write $q_B=\prod_{p\in B}p$, $D=M_0q_B$, and $\mathcal S=\mathcal P_N\setminus B$. Fix the coordinates indexed by $B$ of $\mathcal L_{I,\mathcal P_N,Q}f_s$ at $b=(b_p)_{p\in B}$, obtaining $g_I:X_{\mathcal S}\to\mathbb R$. Let $a'\bmod D$ be the unique residue satisfying $a'\equiv s\pmod{M_0}$ and $a'\equiv b_p\pmod p$ for $p\in B$. Then every frequency $\xi$ on $X_{\mathcal S}$ with $q(\xi)q_B\le Q$ satisfies $$\label{eq:residue-specialization} \widehat{g_I}(\xi)=\frac{D}{\|I\|} \sum_{\substack{x\in I\\x\equiv a'\pmod D}} \mathbf1_A(x)e(-x\xi).$$
  - Explanation: Identifies specialized Fourier coefficients with residue-class restrictions.
- **C21** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-shorter-scale)): For all sufficiently large $N$, every $l\ge1$, and every set $A\subseteq[N]$ avoiding at $l$, the following holds. Let $B\subseteq\mathcal P_N$ satisfy $q_B\le N^\tau$, and put $$D=M_0q_B,\qquad L_D=\lambda(D),\qquad n_B=\left\lceil\frac{N}{L_D}\right\rceil,\qquad \Lambda_B=\frac{L_Dn_B}{N},\qquad \mathcal S=\mathcal P_N\setminus B.$$ For each $v\in V$, choose $s_v\bmod M_0$ and fix the $B$-coordinates of $G_{s_v}$ arbitrarily, obtaining $g_v:X_{\mathcal S}\to\mathbb R$. Then $$\label{eq:shorter-scale} \bigl\|\mathcal Z_{l,\mathcal S}((g_v)_{v\in V})\bigr\| \le\Lambda_B^d\mathcal Y_{lD}(n_B)+O(N^{-\sigma}),$$ uniformly in all these choices. The new scale and normalization satisfy $$\label{eq:shorter-scale-size} N^{1/2}\le M_0^{-k}N^{1-k\tau}\le n_B<N, \qquad 1\le\Lambda_B\le1+M_0^kN^{k\tau-1}.$$
  - Explanation: Compares the multilinear quantity with avoiding sets on shorter intervals.
- **C22** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-descent-cancellation)): For all sufficiently large $N$, all $1\le l\le N^\zeta$, every $A\subseteq[N]$ avoiding at $l$, and every good assignment $(s_v)_{v\in V}$, $$\label{eq:descent-cancellation} \left\|\int\prod_{v\in V}G_{s_v}(z_v)\, d\bigotimes_{p\in\mathcal P_N}(\mu_{l,p}-\varepsilon_{l,p})\right\| \ll N^{-\sigma}.$$ The implied constant is uniform in $l$, $A$, and the assignment.
  - Explanation: Obtains cancellation for good residue assignments on the nonexceptional laws.
- **C23** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-descent-recurrence)): There is a constant $C_1$ such that for every sufficiently large $N$ and $1\le l\le N^\zeta$, $$\label{eq:descent-recurrence} \begin{split} \mathcal Y_l(N)\le {}&C_1N^{-\sigma}+(1+u_N)\biggl[ (1-\rho_l)\mathcal Y_{lM_0}(n_\varnothing)\\ &\hspace{12mm}+\rho_l \sum_{\substack{\varnothing\ne B\subseteq\mathcal P_N\\q_B\le N^\tau}} \eta_{l,B}\mathcal Y_{lD_B}(n_B)\biggr],\\ u_N={}&(1+M_0^kN^{k\tau-1})^d-1=o(1). \end{split}$$ The constant and the sufficiently-large threshold are independent of $l$.
  - Explanation: Recombines cancellation and exceptional terms into a contracting recurrence.
- **C24** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-uniform-descent)): There are constants $a_0,b_0>0$ and $C_0\ge1$, depending only on $h$, such that for all integers $l,N\ge1$, $$\label{eq:uniform-descent} \mathcal Y_l(N)\le C_0l^{b_0}N^{-a_0}.$$
  - Explanation: Proves uniform power decay by induction over the interval scale.

          "exact_quote": "For every prime $p<P_1$, there is an integer $S_p\\ge0$, depending only on $h$ and $p$, such that, for every $l\\ge1$, some integer $x$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:small-prime-argument}\n p\\nmid T_l(x),\\qquad v_p(h_l'(x))\\le S_p.$$ Set $$\\label{eq:local-modulus-exponents}\n E_p=2S_p+1\\quad(p<P_1),\\qquad E_p=1\\quad(p\\ge P_1).$$ For any prime $p$, let $x$ be an integer satisfying <a href=\"#eq:small-prime-argument\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:small-prime-argument\">[eq:small-prime-argument]</a> when $p<P_1$, or representing an allowed regular argument when $p\\ge P_1$. Write $s=v_p(h_l'(x))$. Every integer in the class $x\\pmod{p^{E_p-s}}$ satisfies the unit condition, and its $h_l$-value is congruent to $h_l(x)$ modulo $p^{E_p}$. For each $j>E_p$, the uniform distribution on this argument class modulo $p^j$ pushes forward under $h_l$ to the uniform distribution on $$\\{y\\bmod p^j:y\\equiv h_l(x)\\pmod{p^{E_p}}\\}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-182.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-local-lifting"
    },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-admissible-cycle",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $L>2$ such that $$\\label{eq:cycle-length}\n 4\\delta L>2,\\qquad p^{E_p}\\mid L\\quad(p<P_1).$$ For every $l\\ge1$ and every prime $p$, there are $L$ admissible residues $y_0,\\ldots,y_{L-1}$ modulo $p^{E_p}$, with repetitions allowed, such that $$y_0+\\cdots+y_{L-1}=0\\pmod{p^{E_p}}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-182.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-tuple-laws",
        "exact_quote": "There is a threshold $P>P_1$, depending only on $h$, with the following property. For every $l\\ge1$ and prime $p\\ge P$, there are a probability measure $\\mu_{l,p}$ on $\\mathbb F_p^V$ and a submeasure $0\\le\\varepsilon_{l,p}\\le\\mu_{l,p}$ such that:*\n\n1.  *$\\mu_{l,p}$ is reflection positive. Both $\\mu_{l,p}$ and $\\varepsilon_{l,p}$ are invariant under simultaneous translation of all labels. In particular, any nonzero one of these measures, or their difference, has uniform single-slot marginals after normalization.*\n\n2.  *Writing $$\\eta_{l,p}=\\varepsilon_{l,p}(1),\\qquad\n     \\mu_{l,p}^{\\circ}=\\frac{\\mu_{l,p}-\\varepsilon_{l,p}}{1-\\eta_{l,p}},$$ we have $\\eta_{l,p}\\le p^{-4}$. Under $\\mu_{l,p}^{\\circ}$ every increment $z_{v_{j+1}}-z_{v_j}$, $0\\le j<L$, is admissible at $l$.*\n\n\n3.  *For any integer $D\\ge1$ with $p\\nmid D$ and any $a'\\in\\mathbb F_p$, let $A_{D,a'}$ act on all labels by $z_v\\mapsto\\lambda(D)^{-1}(z_v-a')$. Then $$\\label{eq:tuple-covariance}\n     (A_{D,a'})_*\\mu_{l,p}=\\mu_{lD,p},\\qquad\n     (A_{D,a'})_*\\varepsilon_{l,p}=\\varepsilon_{lD,p}.$$*\n\n\n4.  *For $\\rho_p=\\mu_{l,p}$ or $\\mu_{l,p}^{\\circ}$, every $v\\in V$, and every $f:\\mathbb F_p\\to\\mathbb C$ of uniform mean zero, $$\\label{eq:tuple-mixing}\n     \\left\\|\\mathbb E_{\\rho_p}\n       \\bigl[f(z_v)\\mid(z_u)_{u\\ne v}\\bigr]\\right\\|_{L^2(\\rho_p)}\n     \\le p^{-\\gamma}\\|f\\|_{L^2(\\mathbb F_p)}.$$*\n\n*All thresholds and constants are independent of $l$.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-graph-estimates",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a fixed finite directed graph with no loops and with at most one edge on each unordered pair of vertices. Give its vertices independent uniform labels $Y_x\\in\\mathbb F_p$, and put $$I_G(Y)=\\prod_{x\\to y\\in E(G)}W(Y_y-Y_x),\\qquad e_G=|E(G)|.$$ Then $$\\label{eq:tuple-graph-mean}\n \\mathbb E I_G=c_p^{e_G}+O_G(p^{-4\\delta}).$$ If the vertices are partitioned into two classes and every edge joins different classes, regard $I_G$ as a kernel between their uniform label spaces. Its associated operator satisfies $$\\label{eq:tuple-graph-operator}\n \\|I_G-c_p^{e_G}\\|_{L^2\\to L^2}=O_G(p^{-\\delta}).$$ Both bounds are uniform in $l$.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-signed-holder",
        "exact_quote": "For every positive integer $l$, finite set $\\mathcal P$ of primes at least $P$, and real functions $g,g_v:X_{\\mathcal P}\\to\\mathbb R$, $$Z_{l,\\mathcal P}(g)\\ge0,\n \\qquad\n \\left|\\mathcal Z_{l,\\mathcal P}((g_v)_{v\\in V})\\right|\n \\le\\prod_{v\\in V} Z_{l,\\mathcal P}(g_v)^{1/d}.\n \\label{eq:signed-holder}$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-seminorm",
        "exact_quote": "The map $g\\mapsto\\|g\\|_{l,\\mathcal P}$ is a seminorm on the real functions on $X_{\\mathcal P}$. It satisfies $$|\\mathbb E g|\\le\\|g\\|_{l,\\mathcal P},\n \\qquad\n \\|\\mathbb E_Bg\\|_{l,\\mathcal P\\setminus B}\n =\\|\\mathbb E_Bg\\|_{l,\\mathcal P}\n \\le\\|g\\|_{l,\\mathcal P}.$$ Translations of the input are isometries. Moreover, if $D$ is a positive integer coprime to every prime of $\\mathcal P$, then for any $a'\\in X_{\\mathcal P}$, $$\\left\\|g\\bigl(\\lambda(D)^{-1}(\\,\\cdot-a')\\bigr)\n       \\right\\|_{l,\\mathcal P}\n =\\|g\\|_{lD,\\mathcal P}.\n \\label{eq:lift-transport}$$ Here the affine change is interpreted separately at each prime.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-seminorm",
          "exact_quote": "The map $g\\mapsto\\|g\\|_{l,\\mathcal P}$ is a seminorm on the real functions on $X_{\\mathcal P}$. Moreover, if $D$ is a positive integer coprime to every prime of $\\mathcal P$, then for any $a'\\in X_{\\mathcal P}$, $$\\left\\|g\\bigl(\\lambda(D)^{-1}(\\,\\cdot-a')\\bigr)\n       \\right\\|_{l,\\mathcal P}\n =\\|g\\|_{lD,\\mathcal P}.\n \\label{eq:lift-transport}$$ Here the affine change is interpreted separately at each prime.",
          "source_id": "PM-182.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The map $g\\mapsto\\|g\\|_{l,\\mathcal P}$ is a seminorm on the real functions on $X_{\\mathcal P}$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-large-denominator",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $l\\ge1$ and a finite prime set $\\mathcal P$ as above. At each prime choose either $\\rho_p=\\mu_{l,p}$ or $\\rho_p=\\mu_{l,p}^{\\circ}$. Let $g_v:X_{\\mathcal P}\\to\\mathbb C$ be arbitrary functions. If, in one distinguished slot $v$, every Fourier coefficient at a denominator at most $R$ vanishes, where $R\\ge1$, then $$\\left|\\int\\prod_{u\\in V}g_u(z_u)\\,\n                  d\\bigotimes_{p\\in\\mathcal P}\\rho_p\\right|\n \\le R^{-\\gamma}\\|g_v\\|_2\n                     \\prod_{u\\ne v}\\|g_u\\|_{2(d-1)}.\n \\label{eq:large-denominator}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-182.2"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-large-denominator",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $l\\ge1$ and a finite prime set $\\mathcal P$ as above. At each prime choose either $\\rho_p=\\mu_{l,p}$ or $\\rho_p=\\mu_{l,p}^{\\circ}$. Let $g_v:X_{\\mathcal P}\\to\\mathbb C$ be arbitrary functions. If, in one distinguished slot $v$, every Fourier coefficient at a denominator at most $R$ vanishes, where $R\\ge1$,",
          "source_id": "PM-182.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "C10",
      "kind": "bound",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-large-denominator",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $l\\ge1$ and a finite prime set $\\mathcal P$ as above. At each prime choose either $\\rho_p=\\mu_{l,p}$ or $\\rho_p=\\mu_{l,p}^{\\circ}$. Let $g_v:X_{\\mathcal P}\\to\\mathbb C$ be arbitrary functions. If, in one distinguished slot $v$, every Fourier coefficient at a denominator at most $R$ vanishes, where $R\\ge1$, then $$\\left|\\int\\prod_{u\\in V}g_u(z_u)\\,\n                  d\\bigotimes_{p\\in\\mathcal P}\\rho_p\\right|\n \\le R^{-\\gamma}\\|g_v\\|_2\n                     \\prod_{u\\ne v}\\|g_u\\|_{2(d-1)}.\n \\label{eq:large-denominator}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-182.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-large-denominator"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-lift-moments",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal P$ be any finite set of primes, $Q\\ge1$, and let $I$ be a consecutive integer interval with $|I|\\ge10Q^6$. For every integer $r\\ge1$, every $\\epsilon>0$, every function $f:I\\to\\mathbb C$, and every subfamily $\\mathcal F\\subseteq\\{B\\subseteq\\mathcal P:q_B\\le Q\\}$, $$\\left\\|\\sum_{B\\in\\mathcal F}f_B\\right\\|_{2r}\n \\le C_{r,\\epsilon}\\|f\\|_\\infty Q^\\epsilon.\n \\label{eq:lift-moments}$$ The constant is independent of the prime set, the interval and its location, the function, and the subfamily.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "source_id": "PM-182.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-lift-specialization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B\\subseteq\\mathcal P$, fix $z_B\\in X_B$, and let $G:X_{\\mathcal P}\\to\\mathbb C$. For $1\\le s<\\infty$, $$\\|G(z_B,\\cdot)\\|_{L^s(X_{\\mathcal P\\setminus B})}\n \\le q_B^{1/s}\\|G\\|_{L^s(X_{\\mathcal P})}.$$ In particular, a Fourier lift or a subfamily of its complete supports satisfies <a href=\"#eq:lift-moments\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:lift-moments\">[eq:lift-moments]</a> after specialization with the additional factor $q_B^{1/(2r)}$. The same bound holds after retaining either the remaining denominators at most a given $H\\ge1$, or those greater than $H$.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-zero-free-input",
        "exact_quote": "For every integer $b\\ge1$ and every Dirichlet character $\\chi$ modulo $b$, the Dirichlet $L$-function $L(s,\\chi)$ has no zero in $\\Re s>7/8$.         "source_id": "PM-182.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-prime-progressions",
        "exact_quote": "Uniformly for $Y\\ge2$, $0\\le x\\le Y$, integers $b\\ge1$, and reduced residue classes $c$ modulo $b$, $$\\label{eq:prime-progression}\n \\sum_{\\substack{n\\le x\\\\n\\equiv c\\pmod b}}\\Lambda'(n)\n =\\frac{x}{\\varphi(b)}\n   +O\\bigl(Y^{31/32}\\log^2(2bY)\\bigr).$$ For a nonreduced class modulo $b>1$, the sum is $O(\\log b)$ and has zero main term.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-prime-minor-arcs",
        "exact_quote": "For fixed $k\\ge2$ and $0<\\rho<1$, there is a constant $c_m=c_m(k,\\rho)\\in(0,1/4)$ with the following property. If $Y$ is sufficiently large, $I\\subseteq[1,Y]$ is an interval, and $f\\in\\mathbb R[x]$ has degree at most $k$ with coefficient $b_k$ of $x^k$, then $$\\label{eq:prime-minor-arc}\n \\left|\\sum_{n\\in I}\\Lambda'(n)e(f(n))\\right|\n \\ll_{k,\\rho}Y^{1-c_m},$$ provided $\\theta=(k!)^2b_k$ has an approximation $a/q$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:minor-rational-range}\n (a,q)=1,\\qquad |\\theta-a/q|\\le q^{-2},\\qquad\n Y^\\rho\\le q\\le Y^{k-\\rho}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-182.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-prime-minor-arcs",
          "exact_quote": "For fixed $k\\ge2$ and $0<\\rho<1$, there is a constant $c_m=c_m(k,\\rho)\\in(0,1/4)$ with the following property. If $Y$ is sufficiently large, $I\\subseteq[1,Y]$ is an interval, and $f\\in\\mathbb R[x]$ has degree at most $k$ with coefficient $b_k$ of $x^k$, then $$\\label{eq:prime-minor-arc}\n \\left|\\sum_{n\\in I}\\Lambda'(n)e(f(n))\\right|\n \\ll_{k,\\rho}Y^{1-c_m},$$ provided $\\theta=(k!)^2b_k$ has an approximation $a/q$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:minor-rational-range}\n (a,q)=1,\\qquad |\\theta-a/q|\\le q^{-2},\\qquad\n Y^\\rho\\le q\\le Y^{k-\\rho}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-182.2"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rational-spacing",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $(a,q)=1$, $q\\ge2$, and $|\\theta-a/q|\\le q^{-2}$. For $R\\ge1$ and $M\\ge1$, $$\\label{eq:rational-spacing}\n \\sum_{1\\le r\\le R}\\min(M,\\|\\theta r\\|^{-1})\n \\ll (R/q+1)\\bigl(M+q\\log(2q)\\bigr).$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-prime-differencing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k\\ge2$, and let $f\\in\\mathbb R[x]$ have degree at most $k$, with coefficient $b_k$ of $x^k$.*\n\n\n*If $J$ is an interval of integers of length at most an integer $M\\ge1$, and $m$ is a positive integer, then $$\\label{eq:type-one-differencing}\n \\left|\\frac1M\\sum_{n\\in J}e(f(mn))\\right|^{2^{k-1}}\n \\ll_k\\frac1{M^k}\n \\sum_{|u_1|,\\ldots,|u_{k-1}|<M}\n \\min\\bigl(M,\\|k!b_km^ku_1\\cdots u_{k-1}\\|^{-1}\\bigr).$$*\n\n\n*Next let $A,B$ be positive integers, let $I\\subseteq[1,Y]$ be an interval, and let $|a_m|,|d_n|\\le1$. Set $$S=\\sum_{\\substack{A\\le m<2A\\\\B\\le n<2B}}\n       a_md_n\\mathbf 1_{\\{mn\\in I\\}}e(f(mn)),\\qquad\n \\theta=(k!)^2b_k.$$ Then $$\\label{eq:type-two-differencing}\n \\left|\\frac{S}{AB}\\right|^{2^{2k-1}}\n \\ll_k\\frac1{A^kB^k}\n \\sum_{\\substack{|u_1|,\\ldots,|u_k|<B\\\\\n                  |v_1|,\\ldots,|v_{k-1}|<A}}\n \\min\\bigl(A,\\|\\theta u_1\\cdots u_kv_1\\cdots v_{k-1}\\|^{-1}\\bigr).$$ Zero shifts are included in both sums.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-prime-differencing",
          "exact_quote": "Let $k\\ge2$, and let $f\\in\\mathbb R[x]$ have degree at most $k$, with coefficient $b_k$ of $x^k$.*\n\n\n*If $J$ is an interval of integers of length at most an integer $M\\ge1$, and $m$ is a positive integer, then $$\\label{eq:type-one-differencing}\n \\left|\\frac1M\\sum_{n\\in J}e(f(mn))\\right|^{2^{k-1}}\n \\ll_k\\frac1{M^k}\n \\sum_{|u_1|,\\ldots,|u_{k-1}|<M}\n \\min\\bigl(M,\\|k!b_km^ku_1\\cdots u_{k-1}\\|^{-1}\\bigr).$$*\n\n\n*Next let $A,B$ be positive integers, let $I\\subseteq[1,Y]$ be an interval, and let $|a_m|,|d_n|\\le1$. Set $$S=\\sum_{\\substack{A\\le m<2A\\\\B\\le n<2B}}\n       a_md_n\\mathbf 1_{\\{mn\\in I\\}}e(f(mn)),\\qquad\n \\theta=(k!)^2b_k.$$ Then $$\\label{eq:type-two-differencing}\n \\left|\\frac{S}{AB}\\right|^{2^{2k-1}}\n \\ll_k\\frac1{A^kB^k}\n \\sum_{\\substack{|u_1|,\\ldots,|u_k|<B\\\\\n                  |v_1|,\\ldots,|v_{k-1}|<A}}\n \\min\\bigl(A,\\|\\theta u_1\\cdots u_kv_1\\cdots v_{k-1}\\|^{-1}\\bigr).$$ Zero shifts are included in both sums.",
          "source_id": "PM-182.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $k\\ge2$, and let $f\\in\\mathbb R[x]$ have degree at most $k$, with coefficient $b_k$ of $x^k$.*\n\n\n*If $J$ is an interval of integers of length at most an integer $M\\ge1$, and $m$ is a positive integer, then $$\\label{eq:type-one-differencing}\n \\left|\\frac1M\\sum_{n\\in J}e(f(mn))\\right|^{2^{k-1}}\n \\ll_k\\frac1{M^k}\n \\sum_{|u_1|,\\ldots,|u_{k-1}|<M}\n \\min\\bigl(M,\\|k!b_km^ku_1\\cdots u_{k-1}\\|^{-1}\\bigr).$$*\n\n\n*Next let $A,B$ be positive integers, let $I\\subseteq[1,Y]$ be an interval, and let $|a_m|,|d_n|\\le1$. Set $$S=\\sum_{\\substack{A\\le m<2A\\\\B\\le n<2B}}\n       a_md_n\\mathbf 1_{\\{mn\\in I\\}}e(f(mn)),\\qquad\n \\theta=(k!)^2b_k.$$ Then $$\\label{eq:type-two-differencing}\n \\left|\\frac{S}{AB}\\right|^{2^{2k-1}}\n \\ll_k\\frac1{A^kB^k}\n \\sum_{\\substack{|u_1|,\\ldots,|u_k|<B\\\\\n                  |v_1|,\\ldots,|v_{k-1}|<A}}\n \\min\\bigl(A,\\|\\theta u_1\\cdots u_kv_1\\cdots v_{k-1}\\|^{-1}\\bigr).$$ Zero shifts are included in both sums.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-prime-differencing",
          "exact_quote": "Let $k\\ge2$, and let $f\\in\\mathbb R[x]$ have degree at most $k$, with coefficient $b_k$ of $x^k$.*\n\n\n*If $J$ is an interval of integers of length at most an integer $M\\ge1$, and $m$ is a positive integer, then $$\\label{eq:type-one-differencing}\n \\left|\\frac1M\\sum_{n\\in J}e(f(mn))\\right|^{2^{k-1}}\n \\ll_k\\frac1{M^k}\n \\sum_{|u_1|,\\ldots,|u_{k-1}|<M}\n \\min\\bigl(M,\\|k!b_km^ku_1\\cdots u_{k-1}\\|^{-1}\\bigr).$$*\n\n\n*Next let $A,B$ be positive integers, let $I\\subseteq[1,Y]$ be an interval, and let $|a_m|,|d_n|\\le1$. Set $$S=\\sum_{\\substack{A\\le m<2A\\\\B\\le n<2B}}\n       a_md_n\\mathbf 1_{\\{mn\\in I\\}}e(f(mn)),\\qquad\n \\theta=(k!)^2b_k.$$ Then $$\\label{eq:type-two-differencing}\n \\left|\\frac{S}{AB}\\right|^{2^{2k-1}}\n \\ll_k\\frac1{A^kB^k}\n \\sum_{\\substack{|u_1|,\\ldots,|u_k|<B\\\\\n                  |v_1|,\\ldots,|v_{k-1}|<A}}\n \\min\\bigl(A,\\|\\theta u_1\\cdots u_kv_1\\cdots v_{k-1}\\|^{-1}\\bigr).$$ Zero shifts are included in both sums.",
          "source_id": "PM-182.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-prime-differencing"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-pair-cancellation",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $N$ is sufficiently large, $l\\le N^\\zeta$, and $D=M_0\\prod_{p\\in B}p\\le N^\\beta$, where $B$ is a finite set of primes at least $P$. Let $J_1,J_2:[N]\\to\\mathbb C$ have modulus at most one and be supported on residue classes $a'_1,a'_2\\pmod D$. Assume that $r=a'_2-a'_1\\pmod D$ is admissible at $l$ and that $$J_1(x)J_2(y)\\ne0\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n x<y\\quad\\text{and}\\quad y-x\\notin\\mathcal D_l.$$ Set $$\\Xi=\\{\\xi\\in\\mathbb Q/\\mathbb Z:q(\\xi)\\le H,\n       \\ q(\\xi)\\text{ squarefree},\\ (q(\\xi),D)=1\\}.$$ Then, for the multiplier of any directed cycle edge, $$\\label{eq:pair-cancellation}\n D\\left|\\sum_{\\xi\\in\\Xi}\\Gamma(\\xi)\n       \\widehat{J_1}_{[N]}(-\\xi)\\widehat{J_2}_{[N]}(\\xi)\\right|\n \\ll H^{-\\gamma/2}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-182.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-residue-specialization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $N\\ge1$, $A\\subseteq[N]$, $s\\bmod M_0$, and let $I\\subseteq[N]$ be a nonempty consecutive interval. Let $B\\subseteq\\mathcal P_N$, write $q_B=\\prod_{p\\in B}p$, $D=M_0q_B$, and $\\mathcal S=\\mathcal P_N\\setminus B$. Fix the coordinates indexed by $B$ of $\\mathcal L_{I,\\mathcal P_N,Q}f_s$ at $b=(b_p)_{p\\in B}$, obtaining $g_I:X_{\\mathcal S}\\to\\mathbb R$. Let $a'\\bmod D$ be the unique residue satisfying $a'\\equiv s\\pmod{M_0}$ and $a'\\equiv b_p\\pmod p$ for $p\\in B$. Then every frequency $\\xi$ on $X_{\\mathcal S}$ with $q(\\xi)q_B\\le Q$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:residue-specialization}\n \\widehat{g_I}(\\xi)=\\frac{D}{|I|}\n       \\sum_{\\substack{x\\in I\\\\x\\equiv a'\\pmod D}}\n                       \\mathbf1_A(x)e(-x\\xi).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-182.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-shorter-scale",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $N$, every $l\\ge1$, and every set $A\\subseteq[N]$ avoiding at $l$, the following holds. Let $B\\subseteq\\mathcal P_N$ satisfy $q_B\\le N^\\tau$, and put $$D=M_0q_B,\\qquad L_D=\\lambda(D),\\qquad\n n_B=\\left\\lceil\\frac{N}{L_D}\\right\\rceil,\\qquad\n \\Lambda_B=\\frac{L_Dn_B}{N},\\qquad\n \\mathcal S=\\mathcal P_N\\setminus B.$$ For each $v\\in V$, choose $s_v\\bmod M_0$ and fix the $B$-coordinates of $G_{s_v}$ arbitrarily, obtaining $g_v:X_{\\mathcal S}\\to\\mathbb R$. Then $$\\label{eq:shorter-scale}\n \\bigl|\\mathcal Z_{l,\\mathcal S}((g_v)_{v\\in V})\\bigr|\n \\le\\Lambda_B^d\\mathcal Y_{lD}(n_B)+O(N^{-\\sigma}),$$ uniformly in all these choices. The new scale and normalization satisfy $$\\label{eq:shorter-scale-size}\n N^{1/2}\\le M_0^{-k}N^{1-k\\tau}\\le n_B<N,\n \\qquad 1\\le\\Lambda_B\\le1+M_0^kN^{k\\tau-1}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-182.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-shorter-scale",
          "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $N$, every $l\\ge1$, and every set $A\\subseteq[N]$ avoiding at $l$, the following holds. Then $$\\label{eq:shorter-scale}\n \\bigl|\\mathcal Z_{l,\\mathcal S}((g_v)_{v\\in V})\\bigr|\n \\le\\Lambda_B^d\\mathcal Y_{lD}(n_B)+O(N^{-\\sigma}),$$ uniformly in all these choices. The new scale and normalization satisfy $$\\label{eq:shorter-scale-size}\n N^{1/2}\\le M_0^{-k}N^{1-k\\tau}\\le n_B<N,\n \\qquad 1\\le\\Lambda_B\\le1+M_0^kN^{k\\tau-1}.$$",

The new scale and normalization satisfy $$\\label{eq:shorter-scale-size}\n N^{1/2}\\le M_0^{-k}N^{1-k\\tau}\\le n_B<N,\n \\qquad 1\\le\\Lambda_B\\le1+M_0^kN^{k\\tau-1}.$$",

The new scale and normalization satisfy $$\\label{eq:shorter-scale-size}\n N^{1/2}\\le M_0^{-k}N^{1-k\\tau}\\le n_B<N,\n \\qquad 1\\le\\Lambda_B\\le1+M_0^kN^{k\\tau-1}.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-descent-cancellation",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $N$, all $1\\le l\\le N^\\zeta$, every $A\\subseteq[N]$ avoiding at $l$, and every good assignment $(s_v)_{v\\in V}$, $$\\label{eq:descent-cancellation}\n \\left|\\int\\prod_{v\\in V}G_{s_v}(z_v)\\,\n       d\\bigotimes_{p\\in\\mathcal P_N}(\\mu_{l,p}-\\varepsilon_{l,p})\\right|\n       \\ll N^{-\\sigma}.$$ The implied constant is uniform in $l$, $A$, and the assignment.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-descent-recurrence",
        "exact_quote": "There is a constant $C_1$ such that for every sufficiently large $N$ and $1\\le l\\le N^\\zeta$, $$\\label{eq:descent-recurrence}\n \\begin{split}\n \\mathcal Y_l(N)\\le {}&C_1N^{-\\sigma}+(1+u_N)\\biggl[\n (1-\\rho_l)\\mathcal Y_{lM_0}(n_\\varnothing)\\\\\n &\\hspace{12mm}+\\rho_l\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\varnothing\\ne B\\subseteq\\mathcal P_N\\\\q_B\\le N^\\tau}}\n       \\eta_{l,B}\\mathcal Y_{lD_B}(n_B)\\biggr],\\\\\n u_N={}&(1+M_0^kN^{k\\tau-1})^d-1=o(1).\n \\end{split}$$ The constant and the sufficiently-large threshold are independent of $l$.",
        "source_id": "PM-182.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-descent-recurrence",
          "exact_quote": "There is a constant $C_1$ such that for every sufficiently large $N$ and $1\\le l\\le N^\\zeta$, $$\\label{eq:descent-recurrence}\n \\begin{split}\n \\mathcal Y_l(N)\\le {}&C_1N^{-\\sigma}+(1+u_N)\\biggl[\n (1-\\rho_l)\\mathcal Y_{lM_0}(n_\\varnothing)\\\\\n &\\hspace{12mm}+\\rho_l\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\varnothing\\ne B\\subseteq\\mathcal P_N\\\\q_B\\le N^\\tau}}\n       \\eta_{l,B}\\mathcal Y_{lD_B}(n_B)\\biggr],\\\\\n u_N={}&(1+M_0^kN^{k\\tau-1})^d-1=o(1).\n \\end{split}$$ The constant and the sufficiently-large threshold are independent of $l$.",
          "source_id": "PM-182.2"

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Prime-intersective polynomial** ([source](#sec:introduction)): An integer polynomial $h$ is *prime-intersective* if, for every positive integer $q$, there is an integer $r$ with $$(r,q)=1,\qquad h(r)\equiv0\pmod q.$$
- **Multiplicative scaling function** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Define a completely multiplicative function $\lambda:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$ by $\lambda(p)=p^{m_p}$.
- **Difference set** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a set $A$ of integers, write $A-A=\{a-b:a,b\in A\}$.
- **Exponential character** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Put $e(x)=\exp(2\pi i x)$.
- **Reduced denominator** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For $\xi\in\mathbb Q/\mathbb Z$, let $q(\xi)$ be its reduced positive denominator, with $q(0)=1$.
- **Auxiliary polynomial family** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Put $$\label{eq:auxiliary-definition} T_l(x)=r_l+lx,\qquad h_l(x)=\frac{h(T_l(x))}{\lambda(l)},\qquad a_l=\frac{a l^k}{\lambda(l)}.$$
- **Avoidance at an auxiliary parameter** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A set $A\subseteq[N]$ is *avoiding at $l$* if $(A-A)\cap\mathcal D_l\subseteq\{0\}$.
- **Allowed regular argument** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Call an argument $x\in\mathbb F_p$ *allowed regular* if $$p\nmid T_l(x)\qquad\text{and}\qquad F'(x)\ne0\pmod p.$$

            "exact_quote": "There is a threshold $P>P_1$, depending only on $h$, with the following property. For every $l\\ge1$ and prime $p\\ge P$, there are a probability measure $\\mu_{l,p}$ on $\\mathbb F_p^V$ and a submeasure $0\\le\\varepsilon_{l,p}\\le\\mu_{l,p}$ such that:*\n\n1.  *$\\mu_{l,p}$ is reflection positive. Both $\\mu_{l,p}$ and $\\varepsilon_{l,p}$ are invariant under simultaneous translation of all labels. In particular, any nonzero one of these measures, or their difference, has uniform single-slot marginals after normalization.*\n\n2.  *Writing $$\\eta_{l,p}=\\varepsilon_{l,p}(1),\\qquad\n     \\mu_{l,p}^{\\circ}=\\frac{\\mu_{l,p}-\\varepsilon_{l,p}}{1-\\eta_{l,p}},$$ we have $\\eta_{l,p}\\le p^{-4}$. Under $\\mu_{l,p}^{\\circ}$ every increment $z_{v_{j+1}}-z_{v_j}$, $0\\le j<L$, is admissible at $l$.*\n\n\n3.  *For any integer $D\\ge1$ with $p\\nmid D$ and any $a'\\in\\mathbb F_p$, let $A_{D,a'}$ act on all labels by $z_v\\mapsto\\lambda(D)^{-1}(z_v-a')$. Then $$\\label{eq:tuple-covariance}\n     (A_{D,a'})_*\\mu_{l,p}=\\mu_{lD,p},\\qquad\n     (A_{D,a'})_*\\varepsilon_{l,p}=\\varepsilon_{lD,p}.$$*\n\n\n4.  *For $\\rho_p=\\mu_{l,p}$ or $\\mu_{l,p}^{\\circ}$, every $v\\in V$, and every $f:\\mathbb F_p\\to\\mathbb C$ of uniform mean zero, $$\\label{eq:tuple-mixing}\n     \\left\\|\\mathbb E_{\\rho_p}\n       \\bigl[f(z_v)\\mid(z_u)_{u\\ne v}\\bigr]\\right\\|_{L^2(\\rho_p)}\n     \\le p^{-\\gamma}\\|f\\|_{L^2(\\mathbb F_p)}.$$*\n\n*All thresholds and constants are independent of $l$.",
            "source_id": "PM-182.2"
          }
        ],

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-auxiliary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-auxiliary): Compatible roots and affine substitution -> Integral progression family
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-sums) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-sums): Finite differencing and interpolation -> Local cancellation
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-lifting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-lifting): Derivative valuations and local permutations -> Uniform lifting classes
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-admissible-cycle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-admissible-cycle): Character orthogonality and repetition -> Admissible closed cycles
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-prop-tuple-laws) / [proof](#pm-h-14): Reflection-positive mixture and conditional expectations -> Local tuple laws
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-signed-holder) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-signed-holder): Successive reflection cuts and Cauchy–Schwarz -> Signed multilinear control
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-lem-seminorm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-seminorm): Multilinearity and affine covariance -> Progression-compatible seminorm
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-prop-lift-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-lift-moments): Conditional energy and random-sign symmetrization -> Uniform lift moments
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-prop-prime-progressions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-prime-progressions): Smoothed contour integration and positive differencing -> Prime progression estimates
- **R15** [claim](#stmt-prop-prime-minor-arcs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prime-differencing-2): Vaughan decomposition and Weyl differencing -> Prime minor-arc estimates
- **R19** [claim](#stmt-lem-kernel-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kernel-comparison): Major-arc local factors and minor-arc bounds -> Uniform kernel comparison
- **R18** [claim](#stmt-prop-pair-cancellation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kernel-comparison-2): Plancherel and avoidance -> Pair cancellation
- **R21** [claim](#stmt-prop-shorter-scale) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-shorter-scale): Specialization and progression transport -> Shorter-scale comparison
- **R22** [claim](#stmt-prop-descent-cancellation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-descent-cancellation): Ordered cycle edge and Fourier truncation -> Nonexceptional cancellation
- **R23** [claim](#stmt-prop-descent-recurrence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-descent-recurrence): Exceptional measure expansion -> Contracting recurrence
- **R24** [claim](#stmt-prop-uniform-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-uniform-descent): Simultaneous scale induction -> Uniform power decay
- **R1** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-uniform-descent-2): Density domination at the original parameter -> Main density bound
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-graph-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-graph-estimates): Edge convolution and graph expansion -> Weighted graph control
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-lem-large-denominator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-large-denominator): Exact-support orthogonality and conditional mixing -> Large-denominator decay
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-lem-lift-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lift-specialization): Uniform fiber probability -> Specialized moment bounds
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-thm-zero-free-input) / [proof](#stmt-thm-zero-free-input): Companion theorem input -> Zero-free half-plane
- **R16** [claim](#stmt-lem-rational-spacing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rational-spacing): Rational approximation and separated residue blocks -> Circle-distance bound
- **R17** [claim](#stmt-lem-prime-differencing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-prime-differencing): Repeated Cauchy–Schwarz with translated product domains -> Differencing bounds
- **R20** [claim](#stmt-lem-residue-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-residue-specialization): Character orthogonality -> Exact specialization identity

We have $h_1=h$ and $T_1(x)=x$. For every $D\\ge1$, there is an integer $i\\in\\{0,\\ldots,D-1\\}$ such that $$\\label{eq:auxiliary-change}\n \\begin{split}\n T_l(Dx-i)&=T_{lD}(x),\\\\\n h_l(Dx-i)&=\\lambda(D)h_{lD}(x),\\qquad\n D\\mid\\lambda(D),\\quad \\lambda(D)\\le D^k.\n \\end{split}$$ Moreover, $b_{m_p,p}$ is a $p$-adic unit for all sufficiently large $p$, with a threshold depending only on $h$.",

For every integer $v$ with $p\\nmid v$, $$\\label{eq:local-sums}\n \\begin{split}\n \\left|\\mathbb E_{\\substack{x\\bmod p^j\\\\p\\nmid T_l(x)}}\n e\\left(\\frac{vF(x)}{p^j}\\right)\\right|\n &\\le p^{-8\\delta j} \\qquad(j\\ge1),\\\\\n \\left|\\mathbb E_{\\substack{x\\bmod p\\\\x\\text{ allowed regular}}}\n e\\left(\\frac{vF(x)}p\\right)\\right|\n &\\le p^{-4\\delta}.\n \\end{split}$$ Each expectation is normalized by the number of arguments in its stated averaging set.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-lifting",
          "exact_quote": "For every prime $p<P_1$, there is an integer $S_p\\ge0$, depending only on $h$ and $p$, such that, for every $l\\ge1$, some integer $x$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:small-prime-argument}\n p\\nmid T_l(x),\\qquad v_p(h_l'(x))\\le S_p.$$ Set $$\\label{eq:local-modulus-exponents}\n E_p=2S_p+1\\quad(p<P_1),\\qquad E_p=1\\quad(p\\ge P_1).$$ For any prime $p$, let $x$ be an integer satisfying <a href=\"#eq:small-prime-argument\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:small-prime-argument\">[eq:small-prime-argument]</a> when $p<P_1$, or representing an allowed regular argument when $p\\ge P_1$. Write $s=v_p(h_l'(x))$. Every integer in the class $x\\pmod{p^{E_p-s}}$ satisfies the unit condition, and its $h_l$-value is congruent to $h_l(x)$ modulo $p^{E_p}$. For each $j>E_p$, the uniform distribution on this argument class modulo $p^j$ pushes forward under $h_l$ to the uniform distribution on $$\\{y\\bmod p^j:y\\equiv h_l(x)\\pmod{p^{E_p}}\\}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-182.2"
        }
      ],

      ],
      "method": "Derivative valuations and local permutations",
      "output": "Uniform lifting classes",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-tuple-laws",
      "depends_on": [
        "R3",
        "R5"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-tuple-laws",
          "exact_quote": "There is a threshold $P>P_1$, depending only on $h$, with the following property. For every $l\\ge1$ and prime $p\\ge P$, there are a probability measure $\\mu_{l,p}$ on $\\mathbb F_p^V$ and a submeasure $0\\le\\varepsilon_{l,p}\\le\\mu_{l,p}$ such that:*\n\n1.  *$\\mu_{l,p}$ is reflection positive. Both $\\mu_{l,p}$ and $\\varepsilon_{l,p}$ are invariant under simultaneous translation of all labels. In particular, any nonzero one of these measures, or their difference, has uniform single-slot marginals after normalization.*\n\n2.  *Writing $$\\eta_{l,p}=\\varepsilon_{l,p}(1),\\qquad\n     \\mu_{l,p}^{\\circ}=\\frac{\\mu_{l,p}-\\varepsilon_{l,p}}{1-\\eta_{l,p}},$$ we have $\\eta_{l,p}\\le p^{-4}$. Under $\\mu_{l,p}^{\\circ}$ every increment $z_{v_{j+1}}-z_{v_j}$, $0\\le j<L$, is admissible at $l$.*\n\n\n3.  *For any integer $D\\ge1$ with $p\\nmid D$ and any $a'\\in\\mathbb F_p$, let $A_{D,a'}$ act on all labels by $z_v\\mapsto\\lambda(D)^{-1}(z_v-a')$. Then $$\\label{eq:tuple-covariance}\n     (A_{D,a'})_*\\mu_{l,p}=\\mu_{lD,p},\\qquad\n     (A_{D,a'})_*\\varepsilon_{l,p}=\\varepsilon_{lD,p}.$$*\n\n\n4.  *For $\\rho_p=\\mu_{l,p}$ or $\\mu_{l,p}^{\\circ}$, every $v\\in V$, and every $f:\\mathbb F_p\\to\\mathbb C$ of uniform mean zero, $$\\label{eq:tuple-mixing}\n     \\left\\|\\mathbb E_{\\rho_p}\n       \\bigl[f(z_v)\\mid(z_u)_{u\\ne v}\\bigr]\\right\\|_{L^2(\\rho_p)}\n     \\le p^{-\\gamma}\\|f\\|_{L^2(\\mathbb F_p)}.$$*\n\n*All thresholds and constants are independent of $l$.",
          "source_id": "PM-182.2"
        }
      ],

          "exact_quote": "Suppose $N$ is sufficiently large, $l\\le N^\\zeta$, and $D=M_0\\prod_{p\\in B}p\\le N^\\beta$, where $B$ is a finite set of primes at least $P$. Assume that $r=a'_2-a'_1\\pmod D$ is admissible at $l$ and that $$J_1(x)J_2(y)\\ne0\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n x<y\\quad\\text{and}\\quad y-x\\notin\\mathcal D_l.$$ Set $$\\Xi=\\{\\xi\\in\\mathbb Q/\\mathbb Z:q(\\xi)\\le H,\n       \\ q(\\xi)\\text{ squarefree},\\ (q(\\xi),D)=1\\}.$$ Then, for the multiplier of any directed cycle edge, $$\\label{eq:pair-cancellation}\n D\\left|\\sum_{\\xi\\in\\Xi}\\Gamma(\\xi)\n       \\widehat{J_1}_{[N]}(-\\xi)\\widehat{J_2}_{[N]}(\\xi)\\right|\n \\ll H^{-\\gamma/2}.$$",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-prime-differencing",
      "depends_on": [],

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-prime-differencing",
          "exact_quote": "Let $k\\ge2$, and let $f\\in\\mathbb R[x]$ have degree at most $k$, with coefficient $b_k$ of $x^k$.*\n\n\n*If $J$ is an interval of integers of length at most an integer $M\\ge1$, and $m$ is a positive integer, then $$\\label{eq:type-one-differencing}\n \\left|\\frac1M\\sum_{n\\in J}e(f(mn))\\right|^{2^{k-1}}\n \\ll_k\\frac1{M^k}\n \\sum_{|u_1|,\\ldots,|u_{k-1}|<M}\n \\min\\bigl(M,\\|k!b_km^ku_1\\cdots u_{k-1}\\|^{-1}\\bigr).$$*\n\n\n*Next let $A,B$ be positive integers, let $I\\subseteq[1,Y]$ be an interval, and let $|a_m|,|d_n|\\le1$. Set $$S=\\sum_{\\substack{A\\le m<2A\\\\B\\le n<2B}}\n       a_md_n\\mathbf 1_{\\{mn\\in I\\}}e(f(mn)),\\qquad\n \\theta=(k!)^2b_k.$$ Then $$\\label{eq:type-two-differencing}\n \\left|\\frac{S}{AB}\\right|^{2^{2k-1}}\n \\ll_k\\frac1{A^kB^k}\n \\sum_{\\substack{|u_1|,\\ldots,|u_k|<B\\\\\n                  |v_1|,\\ldots,|v_{k-1}|<A}}\n \\min\\bigl(A,\\|\\theta u_1\\cdots u_kv_1\\cdots v_{k-1}\\|^{-1}\\bigr).$$ Zero shifts are included in both sums.",
          "source_id": "PM-182.2"
        }
      ],

      "method": "Repeated Cauchy–Schwarz with translated product domains",
      "output": "Differencing bounds",

Let $B\\subseteq\\mathcal P_N$, write $q_B=\\prod_{p\\in B}p$, $D=M_0q_B$, and $\\mathcal S=\\mathcal P_N\\setminus B$. Fix the coordinates indexed by $B$ of $\\mathcal L_{I,\\mathcal P_N,Q}f_s$ at $b=(b_p)_{p\\in B}$, obtaining $g_I:X_{\\mathcal S}\\to\\mathbb R$. Let $a'\\bmod D$ be the unique residue satisfying $a'\\equiv s\\pmod{M_0}$ and $a'\\equiv b_p\\pmod p$ for $p\\in B$. Then every frequency $\\xi$ on $X_{\\mathcal S}$ with $q(\\xi)q_B\\le Q$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:residue-specialization}\n \\widehat{g_I}(\\xi)=\\frac{D}{|I|}\n       \\sum_{\\substack{x\\in I\\\\x\\equiv a'\\pmod D}}\n                       \\mathbf1_A(x)e(-x\\xi).$$",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-density**: Prime-intersective polynomial difference avoidance gives a power-saving bound on the size of the integer set.
- **I-local-permutation**: A polynomial with the stated unit slope and integral correction permutes every finite prime-power residue ring.
- **I-zero-convention**: A zero polynomial value imposes no avoidance restriction.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **AU-unit**: Invert the local polynomial with a slope divisible by the prime. -> The inverse can fail to exist or fail to be unique.
- **AU-global**: Treat finite local permutation checks as certification of the density theorem. -> The analytic input and uniform estimates remain unchecked.
- **AU-mapping**: Apply the density bound to arbitrary software states or persistence reduction. -> No correspondence with the required integer set and forbidden differences has been established.
- **AU-zero**: Exclude a set solely because the polynomial vanishes at a prime. -> This would impose a stronger avoidance condition than the theorem states.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The local lifting proof supplies a finite polynomial permutation on prime-power residue rings. The executable construction inverts this local map; it does not certify the density theorem or its companion analytic input.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON p, j and cases containing unit c and ascending coefficients of C.
# PRECONDITIONS: p in {2,3,5}; 1 <= j <= 3; at most 52 cases; c in [-2,2] and p does not divide c; degree(C) <= 2 with coefficients in [-1,1].
# OUTPUTS: For each case, the inverse permutation of G(y)=c*y+p*C(y) modulo p**j, ordered by target residue.
# TIME: O(cases*p**j*j*len(coefficients)) exact integer operations.
# SPACE: O(cases*p**j) exact integers.
# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpz exact integer arithmetic; modular inverse via exact Python integers.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises ValueError; an impossible nondivisible lifting residual raises AssertionError.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz

data = json.load(sys.stdin)
p, j = data['p'], data['j']
cases = data['cases']
if type(p) is not int or p not in (2,3,5) or type(j) is not int or not 1 <= j <= 3 or not 1 <= len(cases) <= 52:
    raise ValueError('outside finite contract')
answer = []
for case in cases:
    c, coefficients = case['c'], case['coeff']
    if type(c) is not int or not -2 <= c <= 2 or c % p == 0 or len(coefficients) > 3 or any(type(a) is not int or not -1 <= a <= 1 for a in coefficients):
        raise ValueError('nonunit or unsupported polynomial')
    def phase(x):
        acc = fmpz(0)
        for a in reversed(coefficients):
            acc = acc*x + a
        return fmpz(c)*x + p*acc
    unit_inverse = pow(c, -1, p)
    row = []
    for target in range(p**j):
        y = fmpz((target*unit_inverse) % p)
        modulus = p
        for level in range(1,j):
            residual = fmpz(target) - phase(y)
            assert residual % modulus == 0
            digit = int((residual // modulus)*unit_inverse) % p
            y += modulus*digit
            modulus *= p
        row.append(int(y))
    answer.append(row)
print(json.dumps(answer, separators=(',',':')))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorem bounds sets of integers avoiding prime-polynomial differences. A representation of software states satisfying these arithmetic hypotheses is ABSENT. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The residue laws are mathematical proof constructions. A game or simulation state model with the required forbidden integer differences is ABSENT. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves arithmetic estimates and density decay. An executable tool contract and effective numerical choices for its thresholds are ABSENT. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof contains finite residue constructions and a descent argument, but an algorithmic specification with a stated computational cost is ABSENT. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Residue products and tuple slots organize the proof. A storage or query contract whose operations satisfy the avoidance hypotheses is ABSENT. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite residue spaces describe arithmetic labels. A hardware execution model or accelerator resource analysis is ABSENT. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Unit residue classes and prime arguments enter the arithmetic avoidance condition. A security experiment, cryptographic construction, or hardness reduction is ABSENT. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Conditional mixing controls functions on residue spaces in the proof. A learning model, training objective, and statistical transfer contract are ABSENT. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The maximal avoiding quantity is used to prove density decay. An optimization procedure, solver contract, and approximation guarantee are ABSENT. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Translation invariance and reflection cuts concern jointly distributed arithmetic labels. A protocol model with the required forbidden integer differences is ABSENT. |
| other (additive_combinatorics) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The paper directly studies the size of integer sets whose differences avoid nonzero polynomial values at primes. Its tuple laws, Fourier lifts, and contracting descent prove a power-saving density bound. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The density bound requires a fixed integral prime-intersective polynomial of degree at least two with positive leading coefficient. A root without the unit condition does not establish the stated hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Applying the density bound to arbitrary polynomials or merely intersective polynomials.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): Prime-intersectivity requires a coprime root modulo every positive integer. Checking only a finite collection of moduli does not establish this universal condition. Blocked use: ['Treating finite modular tests as a certificate of prime-intersectivity.']
- **L3** (edge_case): Zero polynomial values at prime arguments impose no restriction on the set. Only nonzero forbidden differences are excluded. Blocked use: ['Rejecting a set solely because the polynomial has a prime root.']
- **L4** (hypothesis): The prime-distribution argument relies on a companion zero-free half-plane theorem. Local finite computations do not verify this analytic input. Blocked use: ['Claiming that finite residue calculations certify the global prime-distribution input.']
- **L5** (nonconstructive): The final density bound has constants depending on the fixed polynomial; explicit numerical values for these constants are ABSENT. The source incorporates small scales by enlarging the multiplicative constant. Blocked use: ['Using an unspecified exponent or multiplicative constant as a numerical workload guarantee.']
- **L6** (hypothesis): The ordered-pair cancellation estimate requires a sufficiently large scale and bounds on the auxiliary parameter and modulus, together with admissibility and forbidden-support assumptions. Blocked use: ['Using pair cancellation outside its stated parameter range or without its support conditions.']
- **L7** (approximation): A Fourier lift of a bounded interval function need not preserve pointwise bounds. Its moment estimates require the interval-length hypothesis and retention of complete exact supports. Blocked use: ['Treating the lift as a pointwise bounded indicator or dropping the interval-length hypothesis.']
- **L8** (edge_case): Cycle indices may repeat residue labels. The admissible cycle is not asserted to be a cycle of distinct residues. Blocked use: ['Requiring distinct residue labels as part of the admissible-cycle conclusion.']
- **L9** (counterexample_scope): Finite local constructions and exact checks address their specified modular identities. They do not certify the asymptotic density bound or its analytic input. Blocked use: ['Promoting a bounded local test to verification of the full density theorem.']
- **L10** (formal_scope): A mechanized proof of the prime-argument density theorem is ABSENT from the supplied evidence. Blocked use: ['Presenting the prime-argument theorem as already machine-checked.']
- **L11** (unknown): A mapping from engineering states, processes, or matrix columns to an integer set satisfying the forbidden-difference hypothesis is ABSENT. The source supplies no direct correctness or complexity guarantee for such systems. Blocked use: ['Transferring the density theorem to process composition or persistence reduction without a proved mapping.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Let <i>h</i> be a fixed integer polynomial of degree at least two with positive leading coefficient, having a unit root modulo every positive integer. We prove that any set $`A\subseteq\{1,\ldots,N\}`$ whose differences avoid all nonzero values $`h(p)`$ at primes satisfies $`|A|\le C_hN^{1-c_h}`$, where $`c_h\gt 0`$ and $`C_h\ge1`$ depend only on <i>h</i>. Thus the local unit-root condition gives a fixed power saving even when polynomial arguments are restricted to primes. The proof uses the companion zero-free half-plane theorem for Dirichlet <i>L</i>-functions to obtain the required prime-distribution estimates.
